-- Gold decomposition
   Mohammad Ali Khan -> father -> Karim Khan
   Karim Khan -> place of death -> Shiraz

-- Facts
   [Mohammad Ali Khan Zand]
   - Mohammad Ali Khan Zand was the second shah of the Zand dynasty.
   - Mohammad Ali Khan Zand ruled from March 6, 1779 until June 19, 1779.
   - Karim Khan Zand died in 1779.
   - Iran disintegrated after the death of Karim Khan Zand in 1779.
   - Zaki Khan Zand declared Mohammad Ali Khan Zand as the second ruler of the Zand dynasty.
   - Mohammad Ali Khan Zand was the second son of Karim Khan Zand.
   - Mohammad Ali Khan Zand was the son-in-law of Karim Khan Zand.
   - Abol-Fath Khan Zand was the elder son of Karim Khan Zand.
   - Abol-Fath Khan Zand was made the joint ruler of Mohammad Ali Khan Zand.
   - Mohammad Ali Khan Zand had three wives: Anisaah Saad, Hajika Morstaiku, and Korisah.
   - None of Mohammad Ali Khan Zand's wives produced male heirs.
   - One of Mohammad Ali Khan Zand's wives was executed after accusations of fornication.
   - Mohammad Ali Khan Zand died of a heart attack in 1779.
   - Mohammad Ali Khan Zand reigned for less than 5 months.
   - The power was transferred to Abol-Fath Khan Zand after the death of Mohammad Ali Khan Zand.
   [Karim Khan Zand]
   - Mohammad Karim Khan Zand was the founder of the Zand Dynasty.
   - Mohammad Karim Khan Zand ruled from 1751 to 1779.
   - Mohammad Karim Khan Zand ruled all of Iran (Persia) except for Khorasan.
   - Mohammad Karim Khan Zand ruled over some Caucasian lands.
   - Mohammad Karim Khan Zand occupied Basra for some years.
   - Iran recovered from the devastation of 40 years of war during Mohammad Karim Khan Zand's rule.
   - Mohammad Karim Khan Zand provided Iran with a renewed sense of tranquility, security, peace, and prosperity.
   - The years from 1765 to 1779 marked the zenith of Zand rule.
   - Relations with Britain were restored during Mohammad Karim Khan Zand's reign.
   - Mohammad Karim Khan Zand allowed the East India Company to have a trading post in southern Iran.
   - Mohammad Karim Khan Zand made Shiraz his capital.
   - Mohammad Karim Khan Zand ordered the construction of several architectural projects in Shiraz.
   - Civil war broke out in Iran following Mohammad Karim Khan Zand's death.
   - None of Mohammad Karim Khan Zand's descendants were able to rule Iran as effectively as he had.
   - Lotf Ali Khan was the last of Mohammad Karim Khan Zand's descendants.
   - Lotf Ali Khan was executed by Agha Mohammad Khan Qajar.
   - Agha Mohammad Khan Qajar became the sole ruler of Iran.

-- Facts
   [François III d'Orléans, Duke of Longueville]
   - François III de Longueville was born in 1535.
   - François III de Longueville died in 1551.
   - François III de Longueville was the eldest son of Louis II d'Orléans, Duke of Longueville and Marie de Guise.
   - Louis II d'Orléans, Duke of Longueville died on 9 June 1537.
   - François III de Longueville succeeded his father to the duchy of Longueville.
   [Louis II d'Orléans, Duke of Longueville]
   - Louis II d'Orléans, duc de Longueville and comte de Dunois was a French aristocrat.
   - Louis II d'Orléans, duc de Longueville and comte de Dunois was born in 1510.
   - Louis II d'Orléans, duc de Longueville and comte de Dunois died on 9 June 1537.
   - Louis II d'Orléans, duc de Longueville and comte de Dunois was the first husband of Mary of Guise.
   - Mary of Guise later became queen consort of Scotland.
   - Louis II d'Orléans, duc de Longueville and comte de Dunois was the second son of Louis I d'Orléans, duc de Longueville.
   - Louis I d'Orléans, duc de Longueville was married to Jeanne of Hochberg.
   - Louis II d'Orléans, duc de Longueville and comte de Dunois succeeded his brother Claude in 1524.
   - Claude died in 1524.
   - Louis II d'Orléans, duc de Longueville and comte de Dunois married Mary of Guise on 4 August 1534 at the Château du Louvre.
   - Louis II d'Orléans, duc de Longueville and comte de Dunois and Mary of Guise had two children.
   - Louis II d'Orléans, duc de Longueville and comte de Dunois died at Rouen.
   - Mary of Guise later married James V of Scotland.

-- Facts
   [Michael Baden-Powell]
   - Michael Baden-Powell was born on 11 December 1940 in Sinoia, Rhodesia.
   - Michael Baden-Powell is the heir presumptive to the Barony of Baden-Powell.
   - Michael Baden-Powell is the heir presumptive to the Baronetcy of Bentley in the United Kingdom.
   - Michael Baden-Powell is a former accountant.
   - Michael Baden-Powell is a former insurance sales agent.
   - Michael Baden-Powell is the second son of Peter Baden-Powell, 2nd Baron Baden-Powell.
   - Michael Baden-Powell is the brother of Robert Crause Baden-Powell, 3rd Baron Baden-Powell.
   - Michael Baden-Powell is the grandson of Robert Baden-Powell, 1st Baron Baden-Powell.
   - Michael Baden-Powell is the grandson of Olave Baden-Powell.
   - Michael Baden-Powell is the great-grandson of Baden Powell.
   - Michael Baden-Powell's parents moved the family to the United Kingdom in 1949.
   - Michael Baden-Powell was educated at Pierrepont School, Frensham, England.
   - Michael Baden-Powell migrated to Australia in April 1965.
   - Michael Baden-Powell married Joan Phillips Berryman on 20 August 1966.
   - Michael Baden-Powell has three children.
   - Michael Baden-Powell's children are David Robert Baden-Powell, Alexander Peter Baden-Powell, and Myles Warrington Baden-Powell.
   - Alexander Peter Baden-Powell was born in 1973.
   - Myles Warrington Baden-Powell was born in 1975.
   - Michael Baden-Powell resides with his children in the family home Scouts Manor.
   [Peter Baden-Powell, 2nd Baron Baden-Powell]
   - Arthur Robert Peter Baden-Powell, 2nd Baron Baden-Powell, was born on 30 October 1913.
   - Arthur Robert Peter Baden-Powell, 2nd Baron Baden-Powell, died on 9 December 1962.
   - Arthur Robert Peter Baden-Powell, 2nd Baron Baden-Powell, was the son of Lieutenant-General Robert Baden-Powell, 1st Baron Baden-Powell.
   - Lieutenant-General Robert Baden-Powell, 1st Baron Baden-Powell, was the founder of Scouting.
   - Arthur Robert Peter Baden-Powell, 2nd Baron Baden-Powell, was the son of Olave St. Clair Soames.
   - Arthur Robert Peter Baden-Powell, 2nd Baron Baden-Powell, was the nephew of Agnes Baden-Powell.
   - Arthur Robert Peter Baden-Powell, 2nd Baron Baden-Powell, was the nephew of Baden Baden-Powell.
   - Arthur Robert Peter Baden-Powell, 2nd Baron Baden-Powell, was the nephew of Warington Baden-Powell.
   - Arthur Robert Peter Baden-Powell, 2nd Baron Baden-Powell, was the grandson of the Rev. Baden Powell.
   - Arthur Robert Peter Baden-Powell, 2nd Baron Baden-Powell, was named Arthur after his mother's brother.
   - Arthur Robert Peter Baden-Powell, 2nd Baron Baden-Powell, was named Robert after his father.
   - Arthur Robert Peter Baden-Powell, 2nd Baron Baden-Powell, was named Peter after Peter Pan.

   - Edwige Feuillère starred in There's No Tomorrow.
   - George Rigaud starred in There's No Tomorrow.

   - Max Douy designed the sets for There's No Tomorrow.
   - Eugène Lourié designed the sets for There's No Tomorrow.
   [Max Ophüls]
   - Maximillian Oppenheimer was born on 6 May 1902.
   - Maximillian Oppenheimer died on 26 March 1957.
   - Maximillian Oppenheimer was known as Max Ophüls.
   - Max Ophüls was a German-born film director.
   - Max Ophüls worked in Germany from 1931 to 1933.
   - Max Ophüls worked in France from 1933 to 1940.
   - Max Ophüls worked in France from 1950 to 1957.
   - Max Ophüls worked in the United States from 1947 to 1950.
   - Max Ophüls made nearly 30 films.
   - "La Ronde" was released in 1950.
   - "Le Plaisir" was released in 1952.
   - "The Earrings of Madame de…" was released in 1953.
   - "Lola Montès" was released in 1955.
   - Max Ophüls was credited as Max Opuls on several of his American films.
   - "The Reckless Moment", "Caught", and "Letter from an Unknown Woman" were American films directed by Max Ophüls.
   - "The Exile" was an American film directed by Max Ophüls.
   - The annual Filmfestival Max Ophüls Preis in Saarbrücken is named after Max Ophüls.

   - Andrzej Fidyk was born in 1953.
   - Andrzej Fidyk was born in Warsaw, Mazowieckie, Poland.
   - Andrzej Fidyk is a Polish documentary filmmaker and producer.
   - Andrzej Fidyk is best-known for his 1989 documentary "Defilada" ("The Parade").
   - "Defilada" ("The Parade") depicts the mass parades choreographed to celebrate the fortieth anniversary of the Democratic People's Republic of Korea (North Korea) in 1988.
   - Andrzej Fidyk initially planned to be an economist.
   - Andrzej Fidyk studied foreign trade at the Central School of Planning and Statistics at the Warsaw School of Economics from 1972 to 1977.
   - Andrzej Fidyk worked at the Foreign Trade Bureau for two years after graduation.
   - Andrzej Fidyk started working for television in 1980.
   - Andrzej Fidyk has made over 40 documentary films.
   - Andrzej Fidyk's documentary films have been shown primarily on Polish and British television.
   - Andrzej Fidyk worked for the BBC in the Music and Arts Department from 1991 to 1996.
   - Andrzej Fidyk was Head of Documentaries at Polish Television from 1996 to 2004.
   [Defilada]
   - Defilada is a Polish documentary.
   - Defilada was released in 1989.
   - Defilada was directed by Andrzej Fidyk.
   - Defilada focused on the cult of personality in North Korea.
   - Defilada was shot in 1988.
   - 1988 was the 40th anniversary of North Korea's founding.

-- Facts
   [Ragnall Guthfrithson]
   - Ragnall Guthfrithson was a Viking leader.
   - Ragnall Guthfrithson ruled Viking Northumbria in the 10th century.
   - Ragnall Guthfrithson was the son of Gofraid ua Ímair.
   - Ragnall Guthfrithson was the great-grandson of Ímar.
   - Ragnall Guthfrithson was one of the Uí Ímair.
   - Ragnall Guthfrithson ruled Northumbria in 943.
   - Ragnall Guthfrithson ruled Northumbria in 944.

   - Ragnall Guthfrithson's later life is unknown.
   - A "king of the Danes" was reported as being killed by the Saxons at York in 944 or 945.
   [Gofraid ua Ímair]
   - Gofraid ua Ímair was a Viking leader who ruled Dublin and briefly Viking Northumbria in the early 10th century.
   - Gofraid ua Ímair was a grandson of Ímar.
   - Gofraid ua Ímair was a member of the Uí Ímair.
   - Gofraid ua Ímair was probably among those Vikings expelled from Dublin in 902.
   - Gofraid ua Ímair helped his kinsman Ragnall conquer Northumbria.
   - Ragnall died in 920.
   - Sitric Cáech became ruler of Dublin around the same time as Ragnall's death.
   - Sitric Cáech left Dublin to rule in Northumbria in 921.
   - Gofraid ua Ímair succeeded Sitric Cáech as ruler of Dublin in 921.
   - Sitric Cáech conducted raids against the native Irish, including one at Armagh.
   - Sitric Cáech died in 927.
   - Gofraid ua Ímair left for Northumbria in 927 and delegated authority in Dublin to his sons.
   - The sons of Sitric Cáech allied with a "son of Helgi" and seized Dublin.
   - The act of the sons of Sitric Cáech began a period of conflict between the Vikings of Dublin and Limerick which would last until 937.
   - Gofraid ua Ímair's attempt to rule in Northumbria was unsuccessful.
   - Gofraid ua Ímair was driven out of Northumbria by Æthelstan of England within six months.
   - Gofraid ua Ímair returned to Dublin to eject the sons of Sitric Cáech.

-- Facts
   [The Sinking of the Reuben James]
   - "The Sinking of the Reuben James" is a song by Woody Guthrie.
   - The song is about the sinking of the U.S. convoy escort Reuben James.
   - The Reuben James was the first U.S. naval ship sunk by German U-boats in World War II.
   - Woody Guthrie started to write a song including each name on the casualty list of the sinking.
   - The song's chorus is "tell me what were their names".
   - The song is set to the melody of "Wildwood Flower".
   - "Wildwood Flower" is an antebellum tune.
   - "Wildwood Flower" was written by Joseph Philbrick Webster.
   [Woody Guthrie]
   - Woodrow Wilson Guthrie was born on July 14, 1912.
   - Woodrow Wilson Guthrie died on October 3, 1967.
   - Woodrow Wilson Guthrie was an American singer-songwriter.
   - Woodrow Wilson Guthrie was a significant figure in American western folk music.
   - Woodrow Wilson Guthrie wrote hundreds of country, folk, and children's songs.
   - Woodrow Wilson Guthrie wrote ballads and improvised works.
   - Woodrow Wilson Guthrie's album "Dust Bowl Ballads" is included on "Mojo" magazine's list of 100 Records That Changed The World.
   - Many of Woodrow Wilson Guthrie's recorded songs are archived in the Library of Congress.
   - Woodrow Wilson Guthrie was a major influence on songwriters such as Bob Dylan, Phil Ochs, Johnny Cash, Bruce Springsteen, Robert Hunter, Harry Chapin, John Mellencamp, Pete Seeger, Andy Irvine, Joe Strummer, Billy Bragg, Jerry Garcia, Jay Farrar, Bob Weir, Jeff Tweedy, Bob Childers, Sammy Walker, Tom Paxton, AJJ, Brian Fallon, and Sixto Rodríguez.
   - Woodrow Wilson Guthrie frequently performed with the slogan "This machine kills fascists" displayed on his guitar.
   - Woodrow Wilson Guthrie was brought up by middle-class parents in Okemah, Oklahoma.
   - Woodrow Wilson Guthrie's mother Mary was hospitalized as a consequence of Huntington's disease when he was 14.
   - Woodrow Wilson Guthrie's father moved to Pampa, Texas, to repay debts from unsuccessful real estate deals.
   - Woodrow Wilson Guthrie learned folk and blues songs from his parents' friends during his early teens.
   - Woodrow Wilson Guthrie married at 19.
   - Woodrow Wilson Guthrie left his wife and three

-- Gold decomposition
   , also known as Fujiwara no Yoshitsune -> child -> Kujō Michiie
   Kujō Michiie -> child -> Kujō Yoritsune

-- Facts
   [Kujō Yoshitsune]
   - Kujō Yoshitsune was also known as Fujiwara no Yoshitsune.
   - Kujō Yoshitsune was the son of Kujō Kanezane and a daughter of Fujiwara no Sueyuki.
   - Kujō Yoshitsune was a Japanese court noble from the late Heian period to the early Kamakura period.
   - Kujō Yoshitsune held a regent position as Sesshō from 1202 to 1206.
   - Kujō Michiie was the son of Kujō Yoshitsune.
   - Kujō Yoshitsune came of age in 1179.
   - Kujō Yoshitsune was designated as successor of the family in 1188.
   - Kujō Yoshitsune's elder brother died in 1188.
   - Kujō Yoshitsune lost his court position in 1196 due to a political shake-up.
   - Kujō Yoshitsune was appointed to a court position in 1195.
   [Kujō Michiie]
   - Kujō Michiie was a Japanese regent in the 13th century.
   - Kujō Michiie was born in 1193.
   - Kujō Michiie died on April 1, 1252.
   - Kujō Michiie was the father of Kujō Yoritsune.
   - Kujō Michiie was the grandson of Kujō Kanezane.
   - Kujō Michiie was the father of Norizane.
   - Kujō Michiie was the father of Yoritsune.
   - Ichijō Sanetsune was the third son of Kujō Michiie.
   - Ichijō Sanetsune was the founding father of the Ichijō family.
   - Nijō Yoshizane was the second son of Kujō Michiie.
   - Nijō Yoshizane founded the Nijō family.
   - The Kujō family were sponsors of the Kitano Shrine.
   - In 1219, Kujō Michiie offered an emakimono named "Kitano Tenjin Engi Emaki" to the Kitano shrine.
   - In 1223, Kujō Michiie gave an enlarged version of the history to the Kitano shrine.
   - In 1226, Kujō Michiie managed to have his son Yoritsune appointed fourth shōgun of the Kamakura shogunate.
   - In 1238, Kujō Michiie ordained as a Buddhist monk.

-- Facts
   [They All Laughed (song)]
   - "They All Laughed" is a song composed by George Gershwin.
   - The lyrics of "They All Laughed" were written by Ira Gershwin.
   - "They All Laughed" was written for the 1937 film "Shall We Dance".
   - Ginger Rogers introduced "They All Laughed" in the 1937 film "Shall We Dance".
   - Ginger Rogers performed a song and dance routine with Fred Astaire in the 1937 film "Shall We Dance".
   - The lyrics of "They All Laughed" compare those who "laughed at me, wanting you" with those who laughed at some of history's famous scientific and industrial pioneers.
   - The lyrics of "They All Laughed" ask, "Who's got the last laugh now?"
   - Christopher Columbus proved the Earth is round.
   - Thomas Edison invented the phonograph.
   - Guglielmo Marconi developed wireless telegraphy.
   - The Wright brothers made the first flight.
   - The Rockefeller Center was mentioned in the lyrics of "They All Laughed".
   - Eli Whitney invented the cotton gin.
   - Robert Fulton built the "North River Steamboat".
   - Milton S. Hershey created the Hershey bar chocolate.
   - Henry Ford built the "Tin Lizzy" Model T car.
   [George Gershwin]
   - George Gershwin was born Jacob Bruskin Gershowitz on September 26, 1898.
   - George Gershwin died on July 11, 1937.
   - George Gershwin was an American composer and pianist.
   - George Gershwin's compositions spanned both popular and classical genres.
   - "Rhapsody in Blue" is an orchestral composition by George Gershwin, written in 1924.
   - "An American in Paris" is an orchestral composition by George Gershwin, written in 1928.
   - "Swanee" is a song by George Gershwin, written in 1919.
   - "Fascinating Rhythm" is a song by George Gershwin, written in 1924.
   - "I Got Rhythm" is a jazz standard by George Gershwin, written in 1930.
   - "Porgy and Bess" is an opera by George Gershwin, written in 1935.
   - "Summertime" is a hit song from the opera "Porgy and Bess".
   - George Gershwin studied piano under Charles Hambitzer.
   - George Gershwin studied composition with Rubin Goldmark.
   - George Gershwin studied composition with Henry Cowell.
   - George Gershwin studied composition with Joseph Brody.
   - George Gershwin began his career as a song plugger.
   - George Gershwin composed Broadway theater works with his brother Ira Gershwin.
   - George Gershwin composed Broadway theater works with Buddy DeSylva.
   - George Gershwin moved to Paris intending to study with Nadia Boulanger.
   - Nadia Boulanger refused to study with George Gershwin.
   - George Gershwin composed "An American in Paris" after being refused by Nad

-- Facts
   [Anna of Nassau-Dillenburg (1541–1616)]
   - Anna of Nassau-Dillenburg was born on 21 September 1541 in Dillenburg.
   - Anna of Nassau-Dillenburg died on 12 February 1616 in Weilburg.
   - Anna of Nassau-Dillenburg was a daughter of Count William "the Rich" of Nassau-Dillenburg.
   - Count William "the Rich" of Nassau-Dillenburg was married to Juliana of Stolberg.
   - Juliana of Stolberg was the second wife of Count William "the Rich" of Nassau-Dillenburg.
   - Anna of Nassau-Dillenburg married Count Albert of Nassau-Weilburg on 16 June 1559.
   [Juliana of Stolberg]
   - Juliana, Countess of Stolberg-Wernigerode was born on 15 February 1506 in Stolberg, Saxony-Anhalt.
   - Juliana, Countess of Stolberg-Wernigerode died on 18 June 1580.
   - Juliana, Countess of Stolberg-Wernigerode was the mother of William the Silent.
   - William the Silent was the leader of the successful Dutch Revolt against the Spanish in the 16th century.
   - Juliana, Countess of Stolberg-Wernigerode was the daughter of Bodo VIII, Count of Stolberg-Wernigerode and Anna of Eppstein-Königstein.
   - Juliana, Countess of Stolberg-Wernigerode was raised a Roman Catholic.
   - Juliana, Countess of Stolberg-Wernigerode changed her religion to Lutheranism.
   - Juliana, Countess of Stolberg-Wernigerode later changed her religion to Calvinism.
   - Juliana, Countess of Stolberg-Wernigerode and her second husband were convinced Protestants.
   - Juliana, Countess of Stolberg-Wernigerode raised her children in the Protestant ways.
   - Juliana, Countess of Stolberg-Wernigerode's second husband died in 1559.
   - After her second husband's death, Juliana, Countess of Stolberg-Wernigerode remained living at Dillenburg castle.
   - Dillenburg castle belonged to Juliana, Countess of Stolberg-Wernigerode's second son John.
   - Juliana, Countess of Stolberg-Wernigerode kept close to her children throughout her life.
   - Juliana, Countess of Stolberg-Wernigerode was especially close to her son William.

-- Gold decomposition
   William the Younger, Duke of Brunswick-Lüneburg -> father -> Ernest I, Duke of Brunswick-Lüneburg
   Ernest I, Duke of Brunswick-Lüneburg -> place of death -> Celle

-- Facts
   [Ernest I, Duke of Brunswick-Lüneburg]
   - Ernest of Brunswick-Lüneburg was born on 27 June 1497.
   - Ernest of Brunswick-Lüneburg died on 11 January 1546.
   - Ernest of Brunswick-Lüneburg was also frequently called Ernest the Confessor.
   - Ernest of Brunswick-Lüneburg was duke of Brunswick-Lüneburg.
   - Ernest of Brunswick-Lüneburg was a champion of the Protestant cause during the early years of the Protestant Reformation.
   - Ernest of Brunswick-Lüneburg was the Prince of Lüneburg.
   - Ernest of Brunswick-Lüneburg ruled the Lüneburg-Celle subdivision of the Welf family's Brunswick-Lüneburg duchy from 1520 until his death.
   - Ernest of Brunswick-Lüneburg was the son of Henry I, Duke of Lüneburg.
   - Ernest of Brunswick-Lüneburg's mother was Margarete of Saxony.
   - Margarete of Saxony was the daughter of Ernest, Elector of Saxony.
   [William the Younger, Duke of Brunswick-Lüneburg]
   - William VI was born on 4 July 1535.
   - William VI died on 20 August 1592.
   - William VI was Duke of Brunswick-Lüneburg and Prince of Lüneburg from 1559 until his death.
   - William VI ruled together with his brother, Henry of Dannenberg, until 1569.
   - William VI was the son of Ernest I, Duke of Brunswick-Lüneburg.
   - William VI married Dorothea of Denmark on 12 October 1561.
   - Dorothea of Denmark was born on 29 June 1546 in Kolding.
   - Dorothea of Denmark died on 6 January 1617 in Winsen.
   - Dorothea of Denmark was the daughter of Christian III of Denmark and Dorothea of Saxe-Lauenburg.
   - William VI began suffering from fits of insanity in 1582.
   - William VI's wife fled him in 1584 for her own safety.
   - After William VI's death, his wife became regent for their son George.

-- Facts
   [Juan de la Cerda, 4th Duke of Medinaceli]
   - Juan de la Cerda, 4th Duke of Medinaceli, was born around 1514.
   - Juan de la Cerda, 4th Duke of Medinaceli, died in 1575.
   - Juan de la Cerda, 4th Duke of Medinaceli, was a Spanish nobleman.
   - Juan de la Cerda, 4th Duke of Medinaceli, was a Grandee of Spain.
   - Juan de la Cerda, 4th Duke of Medinaceli, was the son of Don Juan de la Cerda, 2nd Duke of Medinaceli.
   - Juan de la Cerda, 4th Duke of Medinaceli, was the son of María de Silva.
   - Juan de la Cerda, 4th Duke of Medinaceli, inherited the titles from his older half-brother Gastón de la Cerda y Portugal in 1552.
   - Gastón de la Cerda y Portugal was the older half-brother of Juan de la Cerda, 4th Duke of Medinaceli.
   - Gastón de la Cerda y Portugal was the 3rd Duke of Medinaceli.
   - Juan de la Cerda, 4th Duke of Medinaceli, was legitimated by the Crown as a legal successor to his father.
   - Don Juan de la Cerda, 2nd Duke of Medinaceli, was the father of Juan de la Cerda, 4th Duke of Medinaceli.
   - Don Juan de la Cerda, 2nd Duke of Medinaceli, was a legitimated bastard.
   - King Philip II of Spain appointed Juan de la Cerda, 4th Duke of Medinaceli, as Viceroy of Sicily in
   [Sancho de la Cerda, 1st Marquis of la Laguna]
   - Sancho de la Cerda, 1st Marquis of la Laguna, was a Spanish nobleman and diplomat.
   - Sancho de la Cerda, 1st Marquis of la Laguna, was born around 1550.
   - Sancho de la Cerda, 1st Marquis of la Laguna, died on 14 November 1626.
   - Sancho de la Cerda, 1st Marquis of la Laguna, was the third son of Don Juan de la Cerda, 4th Duke of Medinaceli.
   - Don Juan de la Cerda, 4th Duke of Medinaceli, was married to Doña Joana Manuel de Portugal.
   - Sancho de la Cerda, 1st Marquis of la Laguna, joined the royal household.
   - Sancho de la Cerda, 1st Marquis of la Laguna, became Spanish Ambassador to Flanders.
   - Sancho de la Cerda, 1st Marquis of la Laguna, was a member of the council of state under King Philip III.
   - Sancho de la Cerda, 1st Marquis of la Laguna, was a member of the council of war under King Philip III.
   - Sancho de la Cerda, 1st Marquis of la Laguna, was head of the household of Queen Margaret of Austria.
   - Sancho de la Cerda, 1st Marquis of la Laguna, was rewarded with the title of "Marquis of la Laguna de Camero Viejo" on 16 February 1599.

-- Facts
   [Lolita (Leah LaBelle song)]
   - "Lolita" is a song recorded by American singer Leah LaBelle.
   - "Lolita" was released on May 7, 2013 through Epic.
   - The single "Lolita" was written by American songwriter Kelly Sheehan in collaboration with record producer Pharrell Williams.
   - "Lolita" is a midtempo funk, R&B, and pop song with disco influences.
   - "Lolita" is backed by synthesizers and a bass guitar.
   - Media commentators have suggested that the song "Lolita" was inspired by Vladimir Nabokov's 1955 novel "Lolita".
   - The lyrics of "Lolita" greatly diverge from the source material of Vladimir Nabokov's novel "Lolita".
   - "Lolita" was positively received by music critics.
   - Music critics praised the chorus of "Lolita".
   - Music critics praised Pharrell Williams' production of "Lolita".
   - Music critics praised Leah LaBelle's vocal performance on "Lolita".
   - "Lolita" ranked number seven on the Dance Club Songs "Billboard" chart in the United States.
   - "Lolita" peaked at number 264 on the official Tophit airplay chart internationally.
   - Leah LaBelle sang an acoustic version of "Lolita" for "Rap-Up".
   - Leah LaBelle performed the original version of "Lolita" at New York Fashion Week.
   - Leah LaBelle performed the original version of "Lolita" at the Club Skirts Dinah Shore Weekend.
   - The music video for "Lolita" was directed by Diane Martel.
   - The music video for "Lolita" features Leah LaBelle as Lolita.
   - The music video for "Lolita
   [Leah LaBelle]
   - Leah LaBelle Vladowski was born on September 8, 1986.
   - Leah LaBelle Vladowski was an American singer.
   - Leah LaBelle Vladowski was born in Toronto, Canada.
   - Leah LaBelle Vladowski was raised in Seattle, Washington.
   - Leah LaBelle Vladowski began to pursue music as a career in her teens.
   - Leah LaBelle Vladowski performed in the Total Experience Gospel Choir during her childhood.
   - Leah LaBelle Vladowski performed in the musical "Black Nativity" during her childhood.
   - Leah LaBelle Vladowski was a finalist on the third season of "American Idol" at age 16.
   - Leah LaBelle Vladowski placed twelfth in the season finals of "American Idol".
   - Leah LaBelle Vladowski attended the Berklee College of Music.
   - Leah LaBelle Vladowski collaborated with Andreao Heard on a demo at the Berklee College of Music.
   - Leah LaBelle Vladowski moved to Los Angeles.
   - Leah LaBelle Vladowski recorded covers of R&B and soul music through her YouTube channel.
   - Keri Hilson hired Leah LaBelle Vladowski as a backing vocalist after seeing her version of "Energy".
   - Leah LaBelle Vladowski worked as a background singer for other artists on their tours.
   - Leah LaBelle Vladowski signed a record deal with Epic in partnership with I Am Other and So So Def Recordings in 2011.
   - Leah LaBelle Vladowski's sampler album "Pharrell Williams and Jermaine Dupri Present Leah LaBelle" was distributed to record companies in 2012.
   - Leah LaBelle Vladowski's sampler album "Pharrell Williams and Jermaine Dupri Present Leah LaBelle" was supported by the single "Sexify".

   - "Of Thee I Sing" is a 1931 song.
   - George Gershwin composed "Of Thee I Sing".
   - Ira Gershwin wrote the lyrics for "Of Thee I Sing".
   - William Gaxton introduced "Of Thee I Sing" in the 1931 musical "Of Thee I Sing".
   - Lois Moran introduced "Of Thee I Sing" in the 1931 musical "Of Thee I Sing".
   - "Of Thee I Sing" was introduced in the 1931 musical "Of Thee I Sing".
   [George Gershwin]
   - George Gershwin was born Jacob Bruskin Gershowitz on September 26, 1898.
   - George Gershwin died on July 11, 1937.
   - George Gershwin was an American composer and pianist.
   - George Gershwin's compositions spanned both popular and classical genres.
   - "Rhapsody in Blue" is an orchestral composition by George Gershwin, written in 1924.
   - "An American in Paris" is an orchestral composition by George Gershwin, written in 1928.
   - "Swanee" is a song by George Gershwin, written in 1919.
   - "Fascinating Rhythm" is a song by George Gershwin, written in 1924.
   - "I Got Rhythm" is a jazz standard by George Gershwin, written in 1930.
   - "Porgy and Bess" is an opera by George Gershwin, written in 1935.
   - "Summertime" is a hit song from the opera "Porgy and Bess".
   - George Gershwin studied piano under Charles Hambitzer.
   - George Gershwin studied composition with Rubin Goldmark.
   - George Gershwin studied composition with Henry Cowell.
   - George Gershwin studied composition with Joseph Brody.
   - George Gershwin began his career as a song plugger.
   - George Gershwin composed Broadway theater works with his brother Ira Gershwin.
   - George Gershwin composed Broadway theater works with Buddy DeSylva.
   - George Gershwin moved to Paris intending to study with Nadia Boulanger.

-- Facts
   [Zita of Bourbon-Parma]
   - Zita of Bourbon-Parma was born on 9 May 1892.
   - Zita of Bourbon-Parma died on 14 March 1989.
   - Zita of Bourbon-Parma was the wife of Charles, the last monarch of Austria-Hungary.
   - Zita of Bourbon-Parma was the last Empress of Austria.
   - Zita of Bourbon-Parma was the Queen of Hungary.
   - Zita of Bourbon-Parma was born as the seventeenth child of Robert I, Duke of Parma.
   - Robert I, Duke of Parma, was the father of Zita of Bourbon-Parma.
   - Infanta Maria Antonia of Portugal was the second wife of Robert I, Duke of Parma.
   - Infanta Maria Antonia of Portugal was the mother of Zita of Bourbon-Parma.
   - Zita of Bourbon-Parma married Archduke Charles of Austria in 1911.
   - Charles became heir presumptive to Emperor Franz Joseph I of Austria in 1914.
   - Archduke Franz Ferdinand of Austria was assassinated in 1914.
   - Charles acceded to the throne in 1916.
   - Emperor Franz Joseph I of Austria died in 1916.
   - The Habsburgs were deposed after the end of World War I in 1918.
   - The empire became four independent countries after the end of World War I in 1918.
   - The four independent countries were Austria, Hungary, Czechoslovakia, and the State of Slovenes, Croats and Serbs.
   - Charles and Zita of Bourbon-Parma left for exile in Switzerland.
   - Charles and Zita of Bourbon-Parma were removed from Hungary by the Allies to Madeira.
   - Charles died in 1922.
   - Zita of Bourbon-Parma was
   [Archduchess Elisabeth of Austria (1922–1993)]
   - Archduchess Elisabeth of Austria was born on 31 May 1922.
   - Archduchess Elisabeth of Austria died on 6 January 1993.
   - Archduchess Elisabeth of Austria was a member of the House of Habsburg-Lorraine.
   - Archduchess Elisabeth of Austria was the youngest daughter of Charles I.
   - Charles I was the last Emperor of Austria.

   - William Marshal, 1st Earl of Pembroke, was born in 1146 or 1147.

   - William Marshal, 1st Earl of Pembroke, served five English kings: Henry II, Henry, Richard I, John, and Henry III.
   - William Marshal, 1st Earl of Pembroke, was knighted in 1166.
   - William Marshal, 1st Earl of Pembroke, spent his younger years as a knight errant and a successful tournament competitor.
   - Stephen Langton eulogized William Marshal, 1st Earl of Pembroke, as the "best knight that ever lived."
   - William Marshal, 1st Earl of Pembroke, became the "de facto" Earl of Pembroke through his marriage to Isabel de Clare in 1189.
   - The title of Earl of Pembroke was officially granted to William Marshal, 1st Earl of Pembroke, in 1199.
   - William Marshal, 1st Earl of Pembroke, was appointed protector for Henry III in 1216.
   - William Marshal, 1st Earl of Pembroke, was appointed regent of the kingdom in 1216.
   - William Marshal, 1st Earl of Pembroke's, father's family held a hereditary title of Marshal to the king.
   - The title of Marshal to the king had become recognized as a chief or master Marshalcy by William Marshal, 1st Earl of Pembroke's, father's time.
   - The title of Marshal to the king involved management over other Marshals and functionaries.
   - William Marshal, 1st Earl of Pembroke, became known
   [Richard Marshal, 3rd Earl of Pembroke]
   - Richard Marshal, 3rd Earl of Pembroke, was born in 1191.
   - Richard Marshal, 3rd Earl of Pembroke, died on 16 April 1234.
   - Richard Marshal, 3rd Earl of Pembroke, was the son of William Marshal, 1st Earl of Pembroke.
   - Richard Marshal, 3rd Earl of Pembroke, was the brother of William Marshal, 2nd Earl of Pembroke.
   - William Marshal, 2nd Earl of Pembroke, died on 6 April 1231.
   - Richard Marshal, 3rd Earl of Pembroke, succeeded William Marshal, 2nd Earl of Pembroke, to the Earldom of Pembroke and Lord Marshal of England.

   - The University of Czernowitz was attended by Edward Bibring until the first World War.
   - Edward Bibring served in the military.

   - Grete L. Bibring was a fellow analyst of Edward Bibring.

   - Grete Lehner Bibring was an Austrian-American psychoanalyst.

-- Facts
   [Margaret Atwood: Once in August]
   - Margaret Atwood: Once in August is a 1984 documentary film.
   - The documentary film is about Canadian writer Margaret Atwood.
   - The film was directed by Michael Rubbo.
   - The film was produced by the National Film Board of Canada (NFB).
   - Michael Rubbo's filmmaking style is characterized as self-conscious documentary filmmaking or metafilm.
   - Michael Rubbo foregrounded the creative process in making the film.
   - Michael Rubbo attempted to uncover autobiographical influences in Margaret Atwood's work while making the film.
   - Michael Rubbo's attempts to uncover autobiographical influences in Margaret Atwood's work were frustrated.
   - Margaret Atwood: Once in August was Michael Rubbo's last film with the NFB.
   [Michael Rubbo]
   - Michael Dattilo Rubbo was born on 31 December 1938.
   - Michael Dattilo Rubbo is an Australian filmmaker, screenwriter, and publisher.
   - Michael Dattilo Rubbo has written and directed over 50 films in documentary and fiction.
   - Michael Dattilo Rubbo studied at Scotch College, Melbourne.
   - Michael Dattilo Rubbo read anthropology at Sydney University.
   - Michael Dattilo Rubbo travelled on a Fulbright scholarship to study film at Stanford University, California.
   - Michael Dattilo Rubbo got his MA in Communication Arts at Stanford University, California.
   - Michael Dattilo Rubbo worked for 20 years as a documentary film director at the National Film Board of Canada.
   - Michael Dattilo Rubbo returned to Australia after working at the National Film Board of Canada.

Q89: Who is the maternal grandmother of Janusz Aleksander Sanguszko?
Gold: Teofila Ludwika Zasławska
NARS answer: Marianna Lubomirska
Lost at: rules miss the answer
Auto-repairs: none

-- Hop plan (the LLM's, from the question alone)
   1. Who is Janusz Aleksander Sanguszko?
   2. Who is the mother of #1?
   3. Who is the mother of #2?
-- Answers of the rule sets voted on: [None, 'Marianna Lubomirska', None]

-- Gold decomposition
   Janusz Aleksander Sanguszko -> mother -> Marianna Lubomirska
   Marianna Lubomirska -> mother -> Teofila Ludwika Zasławska

-- Facts
   [Marianna Lubomirska]
   - Princess Marianna Lubomirska was a Polish noblewoman magnate.
   - Princess Marianna Lubomirska was born in 1693.
   - Princess Marianna Lubomirska died in 1729.
   - Princess Marianna Lubomirska was the heiress of large Ostróg estates.
   - Józef Karol Lubomirski was the Grand Marshal of the Crown.
   - Józef Karol Lubomirski was the son of Aleksander Michał Lubomirski.
   - Aleksander Michał Lubomirski was the Voivode of Kraków.
   - Helena Tekla Ossolińska was a princess.
   - Teofila Ludwika Zasławska was a princess.
   - Teofila Ludwika Zasławska was the daughter of Prince Władysław Dominik Zasławski.
   - Katarzyna Sobieska was the sister of King of Poland Jan III Sobieski.
   - Katarzyna Sobieska was married to Prince Władysław Dominik Zasławski.
   [Janusz Aleksander Sanguszko]
   - Janusz Aleksander Sanguszko was born on 5 May 1712 in Lubartów.
   - Janusz Aleksander Sanguszko died on 14 September 1775 in Dubno.
   - Janusz Aleksander Sanguszko was a magnate in the Polish-Lithuanian Commonwealth.
   - Janusz Aleksander Sanguszko held the title of miecznik.
   - Janusz Aleksander Sanguszko held the title of Court Marshal of Lithuania.
   - Janusz Aleksander Sanguszko was not interested in politics.
   - Janusz Aleksander Sanguszko spent most of his life using the fortunes gathered by previous members of the Sanguszko family.
   - Janusz Aleksander Sanguszko spent his fortunes on his lavish lifestyle.
   - Janusz Aleksander Sanguszko was indebted.
   - In 1753, Janusz Aleksander Sanguszko agreed to divide the "ordynacja ostrogoska" among the members and allies of the "familia".
   - Janusz Aleksander Sanguszko inherited the "ordynacja ostrogoska" from his mother, Marianna Lubomirska.
   - The decision to divide the "ordynacja ostrogoska" was known as the "transakcja kolbuszowska".
   - Hetman Jan Klemens Branicki opposed the "transakcja kolbuszowska".
   - The Sejm (national parliament) accepted the "transakcja kolbuszowska" in 1766.
   - Janusz Aleksander Sanguszko married Konstancja Sanguszko in 1731.
   - Janusz Aleksander Sanguszko separated from Konstancja Sanguszko after their wedding.

-- Facts
   [Fred Frith]
   - Jeremy Webster "Fred" Frith was born on 17 February 1949.
   - Fred Frith is an English multi-instrumentalist, composer, and improvisor.
   - Fred Frith is probably best known for his guitar work.
   - Fred Frith was one of the founding members of the English avant-rock group Henry Cow.
   - Fred Frith was a member of the groups Art Bears, Massacre, and Skeleton Crew.
   - Fred Frith has collaborated with Robert Wyatt.
   - Fred Frith has collaborated with Derek Bailey.
   - Fred Frith has collaborated with the Residents.
   - Fred Frith has collaborated with Lol Coxhill.
   - Fred Frith has collaborated with John Zorn.
   - Fred Frith has collaborated with Brian Eno.
   - Fred Frith has collaborated with Mike Patton.
   - Fred Frith has collaborated with Lars Hollmer.
   - Fred Frith has collaborated with Bill Laswell.
   - Fred Frith has collaborated with Iva Bittová.
   - Fred Frith has collaborated with Jad Fair.
   - Fred Frith has collaborated with Kramer.
   - Fred Frith has collaborated with the ARTE Quartett.
   - Fred Frith has collaborated with Bob Ostertag.
   - Fred Frith composed "Traffic Continues" in 1996.
   - "Traffic Continues" was performed in 1998 by Fred Frith and Ensemble Modern.
   - Fred Frith composed "Freedom in Fragments" in 1993.
   - "Freedom in Fragments" was performed in 1999 by Rova Saxophone Quartet.
   - Fred Frith produces most of his own music.
   - Fred Frith has produced many albums by other musicians.
   - Fred Frith has produced albums by Curlew.
   - Fred Frith has produced albums by the Muffins.
   - Fred Frith has produced albums by Etron Fou Leloublan
   [Rivers and Tides]
   - Rivers and Tides is a 2001 documentary film.
   - Thomas Riedelsheimer directed Rivers and Tides.
   - Rivers and Tides is about the British artist Andy Goldsworthy.
   - Andy Goldsworthy creates intricate and ephemeral sculptures from natural materials.
   - The natural materials used by Andy Goldsworthy include rocks, leaves, flowers, and icicles.
   - Fred Frith composed and performed the music for Rivers and Tides.
   - The soundtrack for Rivers and Tides was released in 2003.
   - The soundtrack is titled "Rivers and Tides (2003)".
   - Rivers and Tides received the ‘Best Documentary’ award of the San Diego Film Critics Society.
   - Rivers and Tides received the ‘Best Documentary’ award of the San Francisco Film Critics Circle.
   - Rivers and Tides is an Anglo-German co-production.
   - Mediopolis Film co-produced Rivers and Tides.
   - Skyline Productions co-produced Rivers and Tides.
   - Skyline Productions is a British independent film company.
   - In 2018, Andy Goldsworthy, Thomas Riedelsheimer, and Fred Frith released a follow-up documentary.
   - The follow-up documentary is titled "Leaning Into the Wind".

   - George Victor was the son of George II, Prince of Waldeck and Pyrmont.

Q108: Who is the paternal grandfather of Johann, Count Of Cleves?

-- Gold decomposition
   Johann was last Count of Cleves -> father -> Dietrich VII, Count of Cleves
   Dietrich VII, Count of Cleves -> father -> Dietrich VI, Count of Cleves

-- Facts
   [Johann, Count of Cleves]
   - Johann was the last Count of Cleves.
   - Johann was Count of Cleves from 1347 through 1368.
   - Johann died in 1368.
   - The counties of Cleves and Mark were united upon Johann's death in 1368.
   - Johann was the youngest son of Dietrich VII, Count of Cleves.
   - Johann's mother was Margaret of Habsburg.
   - Johann succeeded his brother Dietrich VIII, Count of Cleves in 1347.
   - Dietrich VIII, Count of Cleves died without sons.
   - Johann married Mechteld of Guelders.
   - Mechteld of Guelders was the daughter of Reginald II, Duke of Guelders.
   - Mechteld of Guelders died in 1384.
   - Johann's marriage to Mechteld of Guelders remained childless.
   - The County of Cleves went to Adolf III of the Marck after Johann's death.
   - Adolf III of the Marck was a member of the Counts of Marck.
   [Dietrich VII, Count of Cleves]
   - Dietrich VII was Count of Cleves from 1275 through 1305.
   - Dietrich VII was born in 1256.
   - Dietrich VII died in 1305.
   - Dietrich VII was the son of Dietrich VI, Count of Cleves.
   - Dietrich VII's mother was Aleidis von Heinsberg.
   - The County of Cleves was a comital polity of the Holy Roman Empire.
   - The County of Cleves was located in present-day Germany (part of North Rhine-Westphalia) and the Netherlands (parts of Limburg, Noord-Brabant and Gelderland).
   - The rulers of the County of Cleves had a special and privileged standing in the Empire.
   - The County of Cleves was first mentioned in the 11th century.
   - In 1417, the County of Cleves became a duchy.
   - In 1417, the rulers of the County of Cleves were raised to the status of Dukes.
   - The history of the County of Cleves is closely related to that of the Duchies of Jülich, Berg and Guelders and the County of Mark.
   - In 1368, Cleves and Mark were united.
   - In 1521, Jülich, Berg, Cleves and Mark formed the United Duchies of Jülich-Cleves-Berg.
   - The territory of the County of Cleves was situated on both sides of the river Rhine.
   - The capital of the County of Cleves was Cleves.
   - The territory of the County of Cleves roughly covered today's districts of Cleves, Wesel and the city of Duisburg.

   - Boris Karloff starred in Curse of the Crimson Altar.

   - Mervyn Haisman and Henry Lincoln wrote the screenplay for Curse of the Crimson Altar.
   - The screenplay for Curse of the Crimson Altar was based on the short story "The Dreams in the Witch House" by H. P. Lovecraft.
   - Boris Karloff made one of his final film appearances in Curse of the Crimson Altar.
   [Vernon Sewell]
   - Vernon Campbell Sewell was born on 4 July 1903.
   - Vernon Campbell Sewell was a British film director, writer, producer, and actor.
   - Vernon Campbell Sewell was born in London, England.
   - Vernon Campbell Sewell was educated at Marlborough College.
   - Vernon Campbell Sewell directed more than 30 films during his career.
   - Vernon Campbell Sewell's first film was "Morgenrot" (1933).
   - Vernon Campbell Sewell's last film was "Burke & Hare" (1971).
   - Vernon Campbell Sewell worked chiefly in B-movies.
   - Some of Vernon Campbell Sewell's B-movies were well above the usual cut-price standards of film-making at this level.
   - Vernon Campbell Sewell was married to Joan Carol.
   - Vernon Campbell Sewell died on 21 June 2001.
   - Vernon Campbell Sewell died in Durban, South Africa.
   - Vernon Campbell Sewell was 97 years old when he died.

-- Facts
   [Abaqa Khan]
   - Abaqa Khan was the second Mongol ruler of the Ilkhanate.
   - Abaqa Khan was also known as "paternal uncle".
   - Abaqa Khan was transliterated as Abaġa.
   - Abaqa Khan was born in 1234.
   - Abaqa Khan died in 1282.
   - Abaqa Khan was the son of Hulagu Khan and Lady Yesünčin.
   - Abaqa Khan was the grandson of Tolui.
   - Abaqa Khan reigned from 1265 to 1282.
   - Abaqa Khan was succeeded by his brother Tekuder.
   - Much of Abaqa Khan's reign was consumed with civil wars in the Mongol Empire.
   - The Ilkhanate and the northern khanate of the Golden Horde engaged in civil wars during Abaqa Khan's reign.
   - Abaqa Khan engaged in unsuccessful attempts at military invasion of Syria.
   - Abaqa Khan was involved in the Second Battle of Homs.
   [Oljath]
   - Oljath was a Queen consort of Georgia.
   - Oljath was the wife of Vakhtang II.
   - Oljath was the wife of David VIII.
   - Vakhtang II ruled from 1289 to 1292.
   - David VIII ruled from 1292 to 1311.
   - Oljath was a daughter of Abaqa Khan.
   - Abaqa Khan was the Mongol Ilkhan of Iran.

-- Facts
   [Alastair Reid (director)]
   - Alastair Reid was born on 21 July 1939 in Edinburgh.
   - Alastair Reid died on 17 August 2011 in Stoke St Gregory, Somerset.
   - Alastair Reid was a British television director.
   - Alastair Reid directed the TV series "Selling Hitler (1991 miniseries)".
   - The TV series "Selling Hitler (1991 miniseries)" was based on a true story of the biggest fraud in publishing history - the Hitler diaries.
   - Alastair Reid directed the TV series "Traffik".
   - Alastair Reid directed the TV series "Tales of the City".
   - Alastair Reid directed the TV series "Shout at the Devil".
   - Alastair Reid studied at Edinburgh College of Art.
   - Alastair Reid studied directing at the Bristol Old Vic Theatre School.
   - In 1964, Alastair Reid directed episodes of "Emergency-Ward 10" for ATV.
   - Alastair Reid worked regularly in television for over thirty years.
   - Alastair Reid directed the 1997 TV adaptation of Joseph Conrad's "Nostromo".
   [Baby Love (1968 film)]
   - Baby Love is a 1968 British drama film.
   - Alastair Reid directed Baby Love.
   - Ann Lynn starred in Baby Love.
   - Keith Barron starred in Baby Love.
   - Linda Hayden starred in Baby Love.
   - Diana Dors starred in Baby Love.
   - The story of Baby Love tells of a schoolgirl who seduces her adoptive family after her mother commits suicide.
   - Alastair Reid went on to work in television.
   - Linda Hayden was 15 at the time of filming Baby Love.
   - Linda Hayden appeared in "Confessions of a Window Cleaner" (1974).
   - Linda Hayden appeared in "Confessions from a Holiday Camp" (1977).
   - Bruce Robinson made an uncredited appearance in Baby Love.
   - Bruce Robinson directed "Withnail & I" (1987).

-- Facts
   [William X, Duke of Aquitaine]
   - William X, Duke of Aquitaine, was also known as Guillém X in Occitan.
   - William X was called the Saint.
   - William X was Duke of Aquitaine, Duke of Gascony, and Count of Poitou from 1126 to 1137.
   - William X was the son of William IX.
   - William X's mother was Philippa of Toulouse.
   - William X was born in 1099.
   - William X was born in Toulouse.
   - William X's birth is recorded in the "Chronicle of Saint-Maixent".
   - William IX mortgaged Toulouse to Bertrand of Toulouse in 1099.
   - William IX left on Crusade in 1099.
   - Philippa and William X were left in Poitiers after William IX left on Crusade.
   - William IX took up with Dangerose, the wife of a vassal, after returning from Crusade.
   - William IX set aside his rightful wife, Philippa, after returning from Crusade.
   - William X married Aenor de Châtellerault in 1121.
   - Aenor de Châtellerault was the daughter of Dangerose and Aimery.
   - William X had three children with Aenor.
   - William X possibly had a natural son named William.
   - Joscelin is not a son of William X.
   - Joscelin is the brother of Adeliza of Louvain.
   - William X administered his Aquitaine duchy as both a lover of the arts and a warrior.
   - William X raided Normandy in 1136.
   - William X allied with Geoffrey V, Count of Anjou, in the raid on Normandy.
   - Geoffrey V, Count of Anjou, claimed Normandy in his wife's name.
   - William X faced an alliance of the
   [Aénor de Châtellerault]
   - Aénor of Châtellerault was also known as "Aénor de Rochefoucauld".
   - Aénor of Châtellerault was the Duchess of Aquitaine.
   - Aénor of Châtellerault was born in Châtellerault around 1103.
   - Aénor of Châtellerault died in Talmont in March 1130.
   - Aénor of Châtellerault was the mother of Eleanor of Aquitaine.
   - Eleanor of Aquitaine arguably became the most powerful woman in Europe of her generation.
   - Aénor of Châtellerault was a daughter of Viscount Aimery I, Viscount of Châtellerault.
   - Aénor of Châtellerault was a daughter of Dangereuse de L' Isle Bouchard.
   - Dangereuse de L' Isle Bouchard died in 1151.
   - Dangereuse de L' Isle Bouchard was willingly "abducted" by William IX of Aquitaine.
   - Dangereuse de L' Isle Bouchard became the mistress of William IX of Aquitaine.
   - William IX of Aquitaine died in 1127.
   - Aénor of Châtellerault married William X of Aquitaine in 1121.
   - William X of Aquitaine was the son of William IX of Aquitaine.
   - Aénor of Châtellerault had three children with William X of Aquitaine.

-- Facts
   [Mohammed Al-Modiahki]
   - Mohammed Ahmed Al-Modiahki was born on June 1, 1974.
   - Mohammed Ahmed Al-Modiahki is a chess Grandmaster.
   - Mohammed Ahmed Al-Modiahki was the first player in Qatar to earn the title of grandmaster.
   - Mohammed Ahmed Al-Modiahki is Qatar's highest ranked player.
   - Mohammed Ahmed Al-Modiahki was awarded the trophy of the "Player of the Century within the Arab Countries".
   - Mohammed Ahmed Al-Modiahki participated in nine Chess Olympiads.
   - Mohammed Ahmed Al-Modiahki participated in the Chess Olympiads in 1988, 1990, 1992, 1994, 1996, 1998, 2000, 2002, and 2006.
   - Mohammed Ahmed Al-Modiahki has an overall record of (+60,=28,-21) in the Chess Olympiads.
   - Mohammed Ahmed Al-Modiahki won the gold medal for his individual performance on the first board at the 32nd Chess Olympiad in 1996.
   - Mohammed Ahmed Al-Modiahki scored 8/10 at the 32nd Chess Olympiad in 1996.
   - Mohammed Ahmed Al-Modiahki won the gold medal for his individual performance on the first board at the 33rd Chess Olympiad in 1998.
   - Mohammed Ahmed Al-Modiahki scored 7.5/8 at the 33rd Chess Olympiad in 1998.
   - Mohammed Ahmed Al-Modiahki won the bronze medal for his individual performance in 1994.
   - Mohammed Ahmed Al-Modiahki won the bronze medal for his individual performance in 2002.
   - Mohammed Ahmed Al-Modiahki played in the knock out stages of the FIDE World Chess Championships in
   [Zhu Chen]
   - Zhu Chen was born on March 16, 1976.
   - Zhu Chen is a Qatari chess Grandmaster.
   - In 2001, Zhu Chen became China's second women's world chess champion.
   - Xie Jun was China's first women's world chess champion.
   - Zhu Chen became China's 13th Grandmaster in 2001.
   - In 2006, Zhu Chen obtained Qatari citizenship.
   - Since 2006, Zhu Chen plays for Qatar.

   [Mervyn Tuchet, 4th Earl of Castlehaven]
   - Mervyn Tuchet, 4th Earl of Castlehaven, died on 2 November 1686.
   - Mervyn Tuchet, 4th Earl of Castlehaven, was the third son of Mervyn Tuchet, 2nd Earl of Castlehaven.
   - Mervyn Tuchet, 2nd Earl of Castlehaven, was married to Elizabeth Barnham.
   - Elizabeth Barnham was born in 1592.
   - Elizabeth Barnham died around 1622.
   - Mervyn Tuchet, 4th Earl of Castlehaven, married Mary Talbot.
   - Mary Talbot was buried on 15 March 1710/1.
   - Mary Talbot was the daughter of John Talbot, 10th Earl of Shrewsbury.
   - John Talbot, 10th Earl of Shrewsbury, was born before 1601.
   - John Talbot, 10th Earl of Shrewsbury, died in 1654.
   - John Talbot, 10th Earl of Shrewsbury, was married to Mary Fortesque.
   - Mervyn Tuchet, 4th Earl of Castlehaven, had at least three children.
   - Mervyn Tuchet, 4th Earl of Castlehaven, succeeded his brother James as Earl of Castlehaven on 11 October 1684.
   - Mervyn Tuchet, 4th Earl of Castlehaven, held the title of 14th Baron Audley.
   - Mervyn Tuchet, 4th Earl of Castlehaven, held the title of 2nd Baron Audley of Hely.
   - Mervyn Tuchet, 4th Earl of Castlehaven's titles passed to his son James upon his death.
   [Mervyn Tuchet, 2nd Earl of Castlehaven]
   - Mervyn Tuchet, 2nd Earl of Castlehaven, was an English nobleman.
   - Mervyn Tuchet was born in 1593.
   - Mervyn Tuchet died on 14 May 1631.
   - Mervyn Tuchet was convicted of rape and sodomy.
   - Mervyn Tuchet was executed.
   - Mervyn Tuchet was the son of George Tuchet, 1st Earl of Castlehaven and 11th Baron Audley.
   - Mervyn Tuchet's mother was Lucy Mervyn.
   - Mervyn Tuchet was known by the courtesy title of Lord Audley during his father's lifetime.
   - Mervyn Tuchet was knighted by James I in 1608.
   - Mervyn Tuchet studied law at the Middle Temple.
   - Mervyn Tuchet served as Member of the Parliament of England for Dorset in the Addled Parliament of 1614.
   - Mervyn Tuchet was a Justice of the Peace for the counties of Dorset, Somerset, and Wiltshire.
   - Mervyn Tuchet succeeded his father as Earl of Castlehaven and Baron Audley on 20 February 1616/7.
   - Mervyn Tuchet left seven children upon his death.
   - Mervyn Tuchet married Elizabeth Barnham before 1612.
   - Elizabeth Barnham was a sister-in-law of Francis Bacon.
   - Mervyn Tuchet and Elizabeth Barnham had six children.
   - Elizabeth Barnham died in 1622.
   - Mervyn Tuchet married Lady Anne Stanley on 22 July 1624.
   - Lady Anne Stanley was the elder daughter and co-heiress of Ferdinando Stanley, 5th Earl of Derby.

-- Facts
   [Gerald FitzGerald, 3rd Earl of Desmond]
   - Gerald FitzMaurice FitzGerald was born in 1335.
   - Gerald FitzMaurice FitzGerald died in 1398.
   - Gerald FitzMaurice FitzGerald was also known by the Irish Gaelic "Gearóid Iarla" (Earl Gerald).
   - Gerald FitzMaurice FitzGerald was the 3rd Earl of Desmond.
   - The title of Earl of Desmond was located in southwestern Ireland.
   - Gerald FitzMaurice FitzGerald was a member of the Hiberno-Norman dynasty of the FitzGerald, or Geraldines.
   - Gerald FitzMaurice FitzGerald was the son of Maurice FitzGerald, 1st Earl of Desmond.
   - Maurice FitzGerald, 1st Earl of Desmond, was married to Aveline (Eleanor), daughter of Nicholas FitzMaurice, 3rd Lord of Kerry.
   - Aveline (Eleanor) was the third wife of Maurice FitzGerald, 1st Earl of Desmond.
   - Gerald FitzMaurice FitzGerald was the half-brother of Maurice FitzGerald, 2nd Earl of Desmond.
   - Nicholas FitzMaurice was the 3rd Lord of Kerry.
   - Nicholas FitzMaurice was the father of Aveline (Eleanor).
   - Nicholas was the older brother of Gerald FitzMaurice FitzGerald.
   - Nicholas was described as "an idiot".
   - Nicholas was passed over for the earldom due to being described as "an idiot".
   - Some older histories list Gerald FitzMaurice FitzGerald as the 4th Earl.
   [James FitzGerald, 6th Earl of Desmond]
   - James FitzGerald, 6th Earl of Desmond, died in 1462.
   - James FitzGerald, 6th Earl of Desmond, was called 'the Usurper'.
   - James FitzGerald, 6th Earl of Desmond, was the youngest son of Gerald FitzGerald, 3rd Earl of Desmond.
   - Gerald FitzGerald, 3rd Earl of Desmond, was the father of James FitzGerald, 6th Earl of Desmond.
   - Lady Eleanor was the daughter of James Butler, 2nd Earl of Ormond.
   - Lady Eleanor was the mother of James FitzGerald, 6th Earl of Desmond.
